### 🤖 Claude

**C1 is done and merged** — [PR #4](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/4), squashed onto `main` as `673636d` with `pr-checks` green.

**What shipped** — [engine.ts](apps/api/src/services/discounts/engine.ts), a pure `applyDiscounts()` with no I/O and no clock, so E3's checkout totals and C6's preview share one implementation and the §14.3 suite runs without a database. Eligibility (date window, `disabled` veto, usage limit, pre-discount subtotal/quantity minimums), all three discount types with product and collection scopes, applied products → order → shipping with each taking its cut of what's left on the line, order-level discounts split back to lines through `money.allocate()`.

**On the tests** — 27 cases in [engine.test.ts](apps/api/src/services/discounts/engine.test.ts), written first, then checked by mutation rather than trusted:

- The 50-cart property loop found a real bug: a `value: 150` percentage row produced a discount larger than the cart. Fixed by clamping the pool at the eligible base for both value types.
- Dropping the type ordering → 2 red. Good.
- Swapping `allocate()` for naive per-line rounding → **all 27 still passed**. My invariant assertion was tautological (it compared line discounts against a total derived from those same line discounts), and the "uneven" cart I'd picked happened not to drift. Rewrote it to compare allocations against what each discount *told the shopper* it was worth, on a cart that does drift ($9.99 + $9.99 + 2×$12.99, $10 off → naive rounding returns $9.99). That mutation now goes red.

**Two things worth your attention:**

1. **I deviated from the issue on one point.** C1 specifies the invariant `Σ line.totalDiscount === discountTotal - shippingDiscount`, i.e. `discountTotal` includes free shipping. I made `discountTotal` line-discounts-only with `shippingTotal` already net, so E3 computes `subtotal - discountTotal + shippingTotal + tax`. The issue's shape double-counts free shipping in checkout totals, and Shopify shows shipping as "Free" rather than as a discount line — so it loses on both functionality and parity. Logged in `DECISIONS.md` and called out in the PR body for E3.

2. **`amount_off_products` with a fixed value** is one pool across the eligible lines, not "$X off each item". Shopify actually offers the per-item form; SPEC doesn't specify, and the pooled version shares a code path with `amount_off_order` so cents balance identically. If you want per-item semantics, that's a small change and C6's form would need the toggle.

Four other ambiguities (stacking order, ignoring non-entered codes, date-window authority, eligible-lines basis for minimums) are one line each in `DECISIONS.md`. `--passWithNoTests` is off for `apps/api`, and `pnpm verify` is green.

>  * every read and `data: { shopId }` into every write, for every tenant table.

### 🤖 Claude

**C2 is done and merged** — [PR #14](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/14), squashed onto `main` as `401b1aa` with `pr-checks` green.

**What shipped.** `createOrder` is the entry point E3 calls at checkout completion, and the only way an Order row exists — no create endpoint to keep in sync with it. It records rather than prices: totals arrive computed from C1/E3, get asserted to balance, and are stored verbatim. Plus the four admin routes (index with tabs/search/sort/cursor, detail, cancel, timeline notes) and a PATCH for note/tags that C5's order page needs.

**Three things I'd flag:**

1. **The order-number race is the load-bearing bit.** Claimed with `UPDATE … next = next + 1 RETURNING` inside the order transaction so concurrent checkouts serialize on the row lock. The sequence row is created *before* the transaction opens, with `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` — a failed insert inside would roll the whole order back. The mutation check confirms it: swapping to read-then-write makes the concurrent test blow up on the unique index.

2. **Cursor pages tie-break on `orderNumber`, never on `id`.** ULIDs minted in the same millisecond aren't ordered, so an id tie-break drifts the page boundary under exactly the load that makes paging matter.

3. **Two stubs, both logged and both narrow.** `notify.ts` is a no-op seam for the `orders/create`/`orders/cancelled` webhooks because G1 ([#11](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/11)) hasn't landed; `cancel.ts` restocks by hand because B4's adjust service doesn't exist, but it preserves B4's invariant — never move an `InventoryLevel` without writing its `InventoryAdjustment`. Both are called out in `AGENT-LOG.md` with the exact swap point.

**Verification beyond the tests:** I ran it live over HTTP against the seeded stack under `tsx` (autoload resolves routes differently there than under `inject`) — login → index → search `#1041` → detail → note → PATCH → cancel → 409 on re-cancel → `tab=closed` → 404 on unknown id, plus a 403 with the CSRF header missing. It correctly listed and rendered orders another agent had seeded concurrently.

**One process note worth your attention:** the PR sat for ~20 minutes with *no* `pr-checks` run at all. The cause is that GitHub never computes a merge commit for a `CONFLICTING` PR, so `pull_request`-triggered workflows silently never fire — and my only conflict was appended lines in `DECISIONS.md`/`AGENT-LOG.md`. Those files use `merge=union` in `.gitattributes`, which resolves them locally but **GitHub's server-side merge does not apply gitattributes merge drivers**. With ~20 agents all appending to those two files, every PR that sits for more than a few minutes will hit this, and the symptom is silence rather than a red check. Rebasing and pushing fixes it each time. If you want, I can make that self-healing — the cleanest fix is to have the auto-merge workflow rebase-and-push on `DIRTY`, or to split the logs into per-workstream files that never collide.

> #!/usr/bin/env bash
> # Custom git merge driver for pnpm-lock.yaml.
> #
> # A lockfile is a projection of every package.json in the workspace, so a textual
> # 3-way merge is meaningless: it can produce a file that is syntactically valid
> # and semantically wrong. Instead we take OUR side, then regenerate the lockfile
> # from the already-merged package.json files.
> #
> # Invoked by git as: merge-lockfile.sh %A %O %B %P

### 🤖 Claude

Done. Here's where it landed.

**What was actually wrong.** `.gitattributes` marks `DECISIONS.md` and `docs/AGENT-LOG.md` as `merge=union`, and [PARALLEL-AGENTS §5](docs/PARALLEL-AGENTS.md) treated that as load-bearing for the whole 20-agent strategy. GitHub doesn't apply gitattributes merge drivers when computing mergeability. I verified it directly rather than assuming — merging the same two commits with and without `.gitattributes` present, those two files were the *only* difference between "merged clean" and "CONFLICT".

The expensive part isn't the conflict. GitHub builds no merge commit for a conflicting PR, and `pull_request` workflows run against that merge commit — so **`pr-checks` never starts**. The PR doesn't go red. It sits with auto-merge armed and zero checks, looking exactly like slow CI. When I went looking, three PRs were dead that way, including **#19, the admin shell**, which blocks B5/B6/C5/C6/D4/F4/G3/A4. Nothing in the repo would ever have said so.

**What shipped** — [#24](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/24) and [#28](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/28):

- `pr-health.yml` labels every unmergeable PR `needs-rebase` with a sticky comment. It uses `pull_request_target`, which unlike `pull_request` still fires for a conflicting PR, and re-scans *all* open PRs after every push to main.
- `pnpm sync` — rebase onto main (the local union driver resolves the logs) and push. That's the whole fix for an agent.
- A second job that does it automatically, gated to PRs that already have auto-merge on — i.e. whose agent finished and walked away, so it can't interrupt live work.

**I unblocked the backlog by hand** with that bot: #19, #20, #26 and #27 are now mergeable with CI running, from 4-of-4 dead. #11 has a genuine code conflict and was correctly left alone and labelled — that one needs its agent.

**Three things worth knowing:**

1. **The rebase job is inert until you run `gh secret set AGENT_PAT`.** That's deliberate, not unfinished: a push made with the built-in `GITHUB_TOKEN` triggers no workflows, so it would leave branches mergeable and *permanently* unchecked — strictly worse than doing nothing.

2. **This is a treadmill without that secret.** Main moved four times while I was working and every open PR went conflicting again within minutes, because every PR appends to those two files. Labels make it visible; only the bot (or removing the shared files from the hot path, as CLAUDE.md §3 already prescribes) makes it stop. Say the word and I'll split the logs per-workstream — I held off because restructuring canon docs mid-swarm has its own cost.

3. **I found my own bugs by running it, not reading it.** The first version of `pnpm sync` mistook `@{upstream}` for "has been pushed"; the bot skipped the two PRs stuck longest because it filtered on `CONFLICTING` while GitHub was still answering `UNKNOWN`; and a failed checkout was reported to agents as a merge conflict. All three are fixed in #28.

Separately: another agent caught a real bug in my C1 engine ([#22](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/22)) — my `codes[0]` fallback would apply a code-gated discount to a shopper who typed nothing, contradicting my own comment and my own logged decision. Their fix and test are correct.

### 🤖 Claude

**Yes — C3 is done and merged**, as `bf2c1fe` on main ([PR #46](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/46)), `pr-checks` green.

To answer what you saw: I was polling the PR. It sat `CONFLICTING` for ~7 minutes with no checks — the exact treadmill I'd diagnosed. My own `pnpm sync` hit a *real* conflict this time (analytics had added an import to `create.ts` next to mine), I kept both, re-verified, and it landed.

**What C3 shipped** — the order lifecycle is now complete: buy it, ship it, refund it.

- `POST /:id/fulfillments` — validates the remainder first, takes stock through B4's `adjustMany`, derives `fulfillmentStatus` from the lines rather than setting it by hand.
- `POST /:id/refunds/calculate` — the preview C5's refund form shows, computed by the *same function* as the refund, so they can't disagree.
- `POST /:id/refunds` — caps at what's left, refunds shipping at most once, restocks only when asked.
- Order detail now really returns `fulfillments` and `refunds` (C2 left them `[]`), and `cancel.ts` restocks through B4's service instead of writing levels by hand — paying off my own C2 stub.

The refund proration is the part worth knowing: a line's net is split across units with `allocate`, refunding units `[refundedQuantity, +n)`. 2 × $25.00 less a $9.99 discount is $40.01, which doesn't halve — one unit returns $20.01, the next $20.00, totalling exactly $40.01. Naive `net/quantity` returns $20.00 twice and quietly keeps a cent per order. Four mutations confirm the suite catches that and three sibling errors.

**Two things I flagged rather than fixed, both outside my workstream:**

1. **This one breaks the demo.** Refunding a *seeded* order fails with `Unknown transaction mock_ch_1040` — the mock processor's ledger is in-memory per process, so seeded `processorTxnId`s are unknown to the running API. H2 flow (b) is safe (it charges and refunds in one process), but a human clicking **Refund** on any seeded order in the admin hits it. One-line fix in WS-D's `simulated.ts`: treat an unknown txn as captured. Logged for WS-D and WS-H.

2. **E3 will hit a testing constraint I found:** autoload imports route files with a plain dynamic import, so under vitest the route tree and the test file hold separate instances of `@merchant/pay` — separate mock ledgers. Processor-backed refunds are therefore tested against the service, everything else over HTTP. Logged in `DECISIONS.md`.

Remaining C work: **C4** (customers) is unblocked and unclaimed; **C5** and **C6** need the admin shell, which is still in flight. Also, `scripts/stack.sh` has an uncommitted fork-bomb guard from another agent that's been sitting in this worktree all session — not mine, kept out of my PRs, but it'll be lost if nobody lands it. Want me to take C4, or land that stack.sh fix first?

### 🤖 Claude

**Both fixed and merged** as `2f9116c` ([PR #51](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/51)). And you were right about the fork bomb — that guard is already in main; the diff I saw was just my worktree lagging.

**Why I fixed them rather than only flagging.** One was demo-breaking: clicking **Refund** on any seeded order in the admin failed with `Unknown transaction mock_ch_1040`. That's the KPI, and H2 flow (b) would never have caught it because it charges and refunds inside one process. The other was blocking E3 from testing checkout over HTTP at all. Both live in one file, so they were one change.

**What I actually changed, and how I kept it narrow.** My first pass adopted unknown transactions in `refund`, `capture` *and* `voidAuth` — and it broke a WS-D test asserting that capture fails on an unknown transaction. My earlier grep had missed it because the test doesn't use the literal string I searched for. Rather than update their assertion, I checked what the seed actually writes: only `captured`, `partially_refunded`, `voided` — **no authorizations**. So capture-on-unknown is unreachable in the demo, their assertion is correct, and I narrowed adoption to `refund` only. All 143 pay tests pass with WS-D's assertions untouched.

Adoption isn't a hole in the refund cap: `refundPayment` already caps against the sum of `PaymentRefund` rows *before* any adapter is reached, so for a transaction the ledger never issued it has nothing useful to add — and an adopted txn stays refundable rather than being held to a ceiling I'd have to invent, which is what makes a *second* partial refund of a seeded order work.

**Two pieces of evidence, not assertions I wrote to match:**

- Live: refunding seeded order #1040 over HTTP now returns `partially_refunded` with the correct prorated amount. It failed before the change. Seed data restored afterwards.
- The C3 refund tests **moved back onto HTTP** — under #46 they had to call the service directly — and pass. That's the proof for the `globalThis` ledger fix.

The second fix is the one that matters downstream: E3 can now test checkout completion, double-charge idempotency included, through the real route.

One process note: PR #51 went `CONFLICTING` immediately after `pr-checks` went green and sat there for ~4 minutes until I ran `pnpm sync`. That's the treadmill again — it'll keep costing a rebase per PR until `AGENT_PAT` is set, at which point the bot handles it.

Next up on my side: **C4 (customers)** is still unblocked and unclaimed. Want me to take it?

### 🤖 Claude

**C4 is done and merged** — `2d58d64` ([PR #52](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/52)), `pr-checks` green, first try with no rebase needed.

**The two decisions worth your attention:**

1. **`ordersCount` and `totalSpent` are derived, not stored.** The columns exist on `Customer` and I deliberately left them unused — the issue's landmine says so, and it's right: a counter written from three places (order placed, refunded, cancelled) drifts, and the number it drifts into is the one printed next to the customer's name. One `groupBy(customerId, financialStatus)` per page answers both without going per-row. An unpaid order counts as an order but not as money spent.

2. **`findOrCreateByEmail` survives the race**, which matters because E3 calls it at checkout completion — losing a unique-index race there fails a checkout *after* the card was charged. Email is stored and matched case-folded, since Postgres unique indexes aren't.

**On the testing**, one thing is worth reporting honestly. Seven mutations, six went red immediately — but "allow several default addresses" **passed**, because my test had sent one default and one unmarked address, so the normalisation was never actually exercised. I rewrote it to send two addresses both marked default (first wins), then none marked (first becomes default). It fails that mutation now. That's the second time this session a mutation check caught a test that looked fine and proved nothing; it's why I keep running them rather than trusting a green bar.

I also checked the aggregate against raw SQL rather than trusting my own code: for a seeded customer the API says 2 orders / $427.49, and SQL gives `33418 + (27559 − 18228)` = **42749**. Exact, refund netting included.

**One finding for WS-H:** the seed creates *zero* `Checkout` rows, so the admin's "Abandoned checkouts" segment is correctly but visibly empty in the demo. A couple of open checkouts with a seeded customer's email inside 72h would fill that tab. Logged for them.

**Where the C workstream stands:** C1–C4 all done and merged. C5 (orders admin UI) and C6 (customers + discounts admin UI) are both now unblocked — the admin shell landed as PR #19, which is the one the rebase bot rescued earlier. Both are size L and both are pure Polaris UI work against APIs I've already built, so I have the full context for either. C5 feeds H2's smoke flow (b); C6 feeds flow (c) and H3.

Want me to take C5 next?

### 👤 User

c5 is already in progress. make sure c6 is not in progress and has all deps done. then start c6. work on that issue, complete it, and merge it. make sure you follow test driven development with actual good tests where applicable (do not invent unneccessary tests!). make sure you verify and review your code before pushing. make sure you test it. if you have any questions, ask me, but only if they are really important. you can make decisions yourself. you are 10x ultra dev architect and can solve all kinds of problems.

### 🤖 Claude

No `access-control-allow-origin` in the response — the root `.env` is overriding my `ADMIN_URL`, so the browser rejects it. Setting it in `.env` (gitignored) and restarting:

🔧 **Bash**
```
export PATH="$HOME/.nvm/versions/node/v22.23.2/bin:$PATH"
cd /Users/pauljurgens/Downloads/projects/shopify/.claude/worktrees/issue-c1-4553b1
grep -n "^ADMIN_URL\|^API_URL\|^STOREFRONT" .env
python3 - <<'PY'
p='.env'

open(p,'w').write(s)
PY

slee

### 🤖 Claude

My kill was targeting the browser's connection, not the listener — the old API process never died. Killing the actual listener:

🔧 **Bash**
```
export PATH="$HOME/.nvm/versions/node/v22.23.2/bin:$PATH"
cd /Users/pauljurgens/Downloads/projects/shopify/.claude/worktrees/issue-c1-4553b1
echo "listeners before:"; lsof -nP -iTCP:3021 -sTCP:LISTEN -t; lsof -nP -iTCP:3020 -sTCP:LISTEN -t
for p in 3020 3021; do pid=$(lsof -nP -iTCP:$p -sTCP:LISTEN -t); [ -n "$pid" ] && kill $pid; done
sleep 3
echo "listeners after: $(lsof -nP -iTCP:3021 -sTCP:LISTEN -t)$(lsof -nP -iTCP:3020 -sTCP:LISTEN -t)(none if blank)"
(API_PORT=3021 pnpm --filter @merchant/api start > /tmp/c6api.log 2>&1 &)
sleep 6
curl -s -o /dev/null -D - -X OPTIONS http://localhost:30
… [truncated, 147 more chars]
```

> **↳ result**
> ```
> listeners before:
> 12779
> 68459
> listeners after: (none if blank)
> access-control-allow-origin: http://localhost:3020
> ```

### 🤖 Claude

Real bug: my picker double-fires the toggle (ResourceItem `onClick` *and* the inner Checkbox), so clicking does nothing. Polaris has a built-in pattern for this — using it:

### 🤖 Claude

**C6 is done and merged** — `718875e` ([PR #60](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/60)), `pr-checks` green, no rebase needed. **Workstream C is now complete: C1–C6 all landed.**

First, to your questions: C6 was clean — no claim in the log, no branch, no open PR — and its three deps (A3, C4, C1) were all merged before I started.

**What shipped.** Four pages plus the discounts CRUD API the issue folds in. The customer detail edits in place behind one save bar (Shopify has no separate edit screen), and the discounts index's primary action is the split menu of the three types, because the type decides which controls the form even shows.

Two rules live in the service and are worth knowing: **codes are stored uppercase**, so the unique index enforces the case-insensitive matching C1's engine already assumes at checkout — otherwise `WELCOME10` and `welcome10` are two rows and which one applies depends on row order. And **status is derived from the date window at read time**, the same rule as the engine's rejection logic, so the index badge can never say Active while checkout refuses the code.

**Three things worth flagging:**

1. **I deleted code rather than testing it.** A mutation survived: removing the duplicate-code pre-check changed no behaviour, because the unique index already catches it and the handler already maps it. That was redundant code — and a race two concurrent creates could pass — so I removed the pre-check instead of writing a test to protect it.

2. **The browser check earned its keep.** Driving the real pages found a defect nothing else would have: my collection picker rendered its own `Checkbox` inside a `ResourceItem`, so a click fired both handlers and cancelled itself out — **nothing could be selected**. I replaced it with `ResourceList`'s built-in selection, which is the Polaris pattern I should have used first (§7). Also fixed two lists missing React keys. Console is clean on a fresh tab now.

3. **H2 flow (c) needs no setup from me** — `WELCOME10` already exists in the seed and renders Active, which I confirmed on the real index.

Verification was against the seeded stack on an isolated port pair, since another agent owns 3000/3001/3002 — I didn't touch their servers. Customer aggregates matched what I'd verified against raw SQL in C4 (Simone: 2 orders / $427.49). All test data was removed; the seed is back to its three discounts.

C5 landed while I was working, so the whole C workstream is closed out. If you want me to keep going, the useful next moves are picking up whatever's still unclaimed in `docs/issues/INDEX.md` — or setting `AGENT_PAT` so the rebase bot stops making every agent hand-rebase.
